{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Base directory for this skill: /home/codex/repos/ns006-1405-impleval/.claude/skills/netscript-harness\n\n# NetScript Harness (V3) — Orchestration Skill\n\nThis skill coordinates harness-mode runs. The authoritative harness docs live under `.llm/harness/`;\nthis skill tells you what to load and in what order.\n\nAgent lanes and their model bindings are configuration, not dogma — the single source of truth is\n[`workflow/lane-policy.md`](../../../.llm/harness/workflow/lane-policy.md). Only two rules are hard:\n**generator-session ≠ evaluator-session**, and the **slice review gate** (no implementation lane\nself-certifies). Everything else is a per-run lane assignment recorded in the run dir's\n`supervisor.md`/`drift.md`.\n\n## When to Use\n\n- The user says `use harness` or asks for a harnessed run.\n- Selecting archetypes, scope overlays, or gate sets.\n- Tracking run artifacts, commits, or drift.\n- Understanding evaluator protocol (PLAN-EVAL or IMPL-EVAL).\n- Deciding where a lesson or doctrine update should live.\n\n## When Not to Use\n\n- For package/plugin architecture decisions — use `netscript-doctrine`.\n- For JSR readiness audits — use `jsr-audit`.\n- For frontend/framework-specific questions — use `deno-fresh` or the relevant domain skill.\n\n## Key Concepts\n\n| Concept           | Meaning                                                                                 |\n| ----------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| **9-phase model** | Bootstrap → Research → Plan & Design → Plan-Gate → Implement → Gate → Evaluate → Release → Close. |\n| **PLAN-EVAL**     | Conditional pre-implementation pass for complex/decision-heavy work; hard stop when selected. |\n| **IMPL-EVAL**     | Mandatory final pass after implementation unless the owner explicitly waives it.       |\n| **Tiered lanes**  | Agent lanes A–E with named model bindings; single source `workflow/lane-policy.md`.     |\n| **Slice review gate** | Tier-A supervisor substantively reviews each landed slice before the sign-off commit; no lane self-certifies (A1). |\n| **Supervisor identity** | Every run dir carries `supervisor.md` (model, session, host, paths, branch, baseline, lanes). |\n| **Plan-Gate**     | Checklist (`gates/plan-gate.md`) that PLAN-EVAL enforces.                               |\n| **Archetype**     | Package/plugin shape profile from `archetypes/ARCHETYPE-*.md`.                          |\n| **Scope overlay** | `SCOPE-frontend.md`, `SCOPE-service.md`, `SCOPE-docs.md`.                               |\n| **Seed run**      | Planning-only run shape (`workflow/seed-run.md`): discovery → roadmap → owner-ratified one-shot GitHub filing. Use when the deliverable is a board (epics + issues), not code. |\n| **Run artifact**  | File in `.llm/runs/<run-id>/` that preserves state across sessions.                     |\n| **Debt**          | Recorded in `.llm/harness/debt/arch-debt.md`.                                           |\n\nFor a **supervisor run** (two or more capability-scoped phase groups), also read\n`.llm/harness/workflow/supervisor.md` and `.llm/harness/workflow/escalation.md`, and track the\ngroups in `phase-registry.md`.\n\nFor OpenHands or local-agent handoffs during a run, also read\n`.llm/harness/workflow/agent-handoff.md` and `.agents/skills/openhands-handoff/SKILL.md`.\n\n## Workflow\n\nThe user may still write `profile: package`, `profile: docs`, or similar. In V3 that field is an\nintent hint, not the final profile.\n\n| User hint                  | V3 selection                                                |\n| -------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------- |\n| `package`                  | identify `ARCHETYPE-1` through `ARCHETYPE-6`                |\n| `plugin`                   | normally `ARCHETYPE-5`, unless sibling packages also change |\n| `frontend`                 | affected archetype(s) plus `SCOPE-frontend.md`              |\n| `service`                  | affected archetype(s) plus `SCOPE-service.md`               |\n| `docs` or `knowledge-base` | `SCOPE-docs.md` plus any described archetypes               |\n\n1. Read `workflow/activation.md` and `workflow/run-loop.md`.\n2. If resuming, read `.llm/runs/<run-id>/context-pack.md`.\n3. Identify the target surface and select archetype + overlays.\n4. Read `gates/archetype-gate-matrix.md` and `gates/plan-gate.md`.\n5. Scaffold run artifacts from `templates/`.\n6. Produce `research.md`, then `plan.md` with locked decisions.\n7. Record Design checkpoint in `worklog.md`.\n8. **For complex/decision-heavy work, run PLAN-EVAL in a separate session before implementation;\n   otherwise record a justified `PLAN-EVAL: N/A`.**\n9. Implement one commit slice at a time; push + comment on the draft PR (the commit trail) and keep\n   `worklog.md`/`context-pack.md` current after each.\n10. Run gates; record results in `worklog.md`.\n11. **Run IMPL-EVAL (separate session) unless the owner explicitly waives it.**\n12. Close: update `context-pack.md`, `arch-debt.md`, and promote lessons if warranted.\n\nWhen the opening diff is docs-only, proactively apply `ci:skip-e2e` to the draft PR and also apply\n`ci:skip-scaffold` when scaffold-static is not applicable. Use `ci:full` as the escape hatch when\nthe docs-only change must exercise the full CI surface. Record the selection in the opening phase\ncomment so the evaluator can verify that the cheap lane is intentional.\n\n## Agent Delegation Contract\n\nLane assignments and model bindings are configuration. The tiered A–E model, the selection rules\n(source slices → Tier D, batch/parallel → Tier C with committed `workflow.js`, research/doc prose →\nTier B), and the named model bindings are defined once in\n[`workflow/lane-policy.md`](../../../.llm/harness/workflow/lane-policy.md). Do not restate lane\nrouting here — defer to that file. The items below are the parts of the contract that hold as\n**invariants** regardless of which lane implements:\n\n- **Generator ≠ evaluator (hard invariant).** The session that generates a plan or an\n  implementation is never the session that evaluates it. Which model implements is configurable per\n  run and recorded in `supervisor.md`/`drift.md`; the session separation is not.\n- **Slice review gate (A1, hard invariant).** After any implementation lane lands a slice and its\n  automated gates pass, the Tier-A supervisor substantively reviews the slice content before the\n  sign-off commit — and the sign-off commit is the supervisor's, not the implementer's. This holds\n  for every implementation tier (B Opus sub-agents, C Workflow-generated slices, D WSL Codex); no\n  lane self-certifies. See `workflow/lane-policy.md` for the rule and `workflow/run-loop.md` for the\n  step placement.\n- **Evaluator route binding.** Select the evaluator route from `workflow/lane-policy.md` — the\n  open-model evaluator lane for a formal PLAN-EVAL/IMPL-EVAL, or the opposite-family route for\n  ordinary review — and record it in the run. The two-failure eval loop is unchanged.\n- **Tier-D mobile-visibility proof.** A Tier-D (WSL Codex) implementation slice is launched only via\n  skills + `.llm/tools/agentic/` (never ad-hoc `wsl.exe`), and only when the run artifacts include\n  the WSL worktree path, concrete Codex thread id, daemon-managed `remote-control` proof, and the\n  follow-up steering command for that same thread. Without those, record the launch as\n  failed/not-attached. Claude helpers (`codex:rescue`, `codex:codex-rescue`, `codex-companion.mjs`,\n  internal `general-purpose` agents) are local tool surfaces, not daemon-attached Codex threads.\n- **Per-slice trackability.** Every implementation slice is independently trackable: branch/worktree\n  identity, agent/thread identity, files touched, tests run, commit hash, push status, and PR\n  comment/status. Each slice commits, pushes, and comments on the draft PR before the next slice is\n  considered complete (the draft-PR commit list + per-slice PR comments are the commit trail).\n- **Green-gate merge bar.** Merge, publish, or release gates require all relevant tests green with\n  required features intact. For catalog-related work, do not delete, skip, de-catalog, or bypass\n  tests unless the evaluator verdict explicitly classifies the test as stale/irrelevant and the PR\n  comment records the rationale.\n- **Blocked-lane handling.** If an evaluator or implementation launch path is blocked, record the\n  missing launch mechanism in `worklog.md`/`drift.md`, then proceed only with an owner-authorized\n  fallback lane recorded in `supervisor.md`/`drift.md`.\n\n## Common Pitfalls\n\n- **Skipping a selected Plan-Gate** — for complex/decision-heavy work, implementation before\n  PLAN-EVAL `PASS` is a process failure. Small/mechanical work records `PLAN-EVAL: N/A` first.\n- **Self-evaluation** — The evaluator must be a separate session. The generator does not\n  self-certify.\n- **Wrong evaluator surface** — the generator session may never evaluate its own output. For a\n  **local-machine run**, PLAN-EVAL and IMPL-EVAL normally use a fresh native opposite-family\n  session: Claude/Fable evaluates Codex-authored work and Codex/Sol evaluates Claude-authored work.\n  Use the phase-bound Minimax/DeepSeek OpenRouter preset only for a genuine third opinion or when\n  the native opposite-family route is quota-blocked. If OpenRouter is then limited, use a fresh AGY\n  Gemini 3.6 Flash high session on the Google subscription. OpenHands is reserved for explicitly\n  cloud-driven work. The **supervisor chooses when to trigger**; never auto-dispatch an evaluator\n  from a sub-agent. Record every blocked route, escalation, and requested/observed identity.\n- **Self-certifying a slice** — a green automated gate is not a sign-off. The Tier-A supervisor must\n  substantively review the slice before the sign-off commit, for every implementation lane\n  (`workflow/lane-policy.md` invariant 2). No lane self-certifies.\n- **Treating lanes as dogma** — the \"Claude coordinates / OpenHands evaluates / Codex implements\"\n  fixed-lane assertion is retired. Lanes are configuration; consult `workflow/lane-policy.md` and\n  honor the run's recorded lane assignments/overrides in `supervisor.md`/`drift.md`.\n- **False attached-agent claims** — for Tier-D slices, a Claude `codex:*` skill/helper is not a WSL\n  Codex daemon thread. Require daemon status plus thread id before claiming the user can see or steer\n  the subagent from phone/Desktop.\n- **Carried-in plans as ground truth** — Re-baseline against current `main` before locking the plan.\n- **Monolithic commits** — Commit by slice, not by monolith. Each slice has its own gate.\n- **Raw root CLI noise as a verdict** — Package-quality check/lint/fmt evidence must come from the\n  scoped wrappers, not raw root `deno fmt --check` over Markdown, generated targets, or legacy\n  line-ending drift (which is not a package-quality verdict source unless the plan explicitly owns\n  repo-wide formatting). The wrapper invocations and verdict rules live in\n  `.agents/skills/netscript-tools`.\n- **Scoped wrappers as a full quality verdict** — the scoped check/lint/fmt wrappers pass code\n  containing `any` and honor inline `// deno-lint-ignore no-explicit-any`, so a green wrapper run\n  does NOT prove doctrine/jsr compliance. For any slice touching `packages/**` or `plugins/**`, the\n  Tier-A slice review MUST additionally run `deno task quality:scan` (fails on `any`+casting and\n  host-side hardcoded plugin names — the two classes that reached `main` in #745) and\n  `deno task arch:check` (doctrine fitness), plus the jsr-audit skill for package/plugin waves. A\n  new `// deno-lint-ignore` or `as unknown as` introduced to green a wrapper is a review-blocking\n  finding, not a pass. Reviewing on wrappers alone is the exact hole that let #745 merge.\n\n## Run Artifacts\n\nRun artifacts live under `.llm/runs/<run-id>/` and use templates from `.llm/harness/templates/`.\n\n`<run-id>` is the current branch name with `/` replaced by `-`, followed by `--<suffix>`.\n\n| File                | Purpose                                                     |\n| ------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------- |\n| `supervisor.md`     | supervisor identity (model, session, host, paths, branch, baseline, lanes) — written at run start |\n| `research.md`       | deep findings, re-baseline of carried-in plans              |\n| `plan.md`           | approved scope, archetype, gates, debt implications         |\n| `implement.md`      | generator prompt when needed                                |\n| `worklog.md`        | implementation evidence and gate results                    |\n| `plan-eval.md`      | PLAN-EVAL verdict (separate session, before implementation) |\n| `evaluate.md`       | IMPL-EVAL verdict (separate session, after implementation)  |\n| `context-pack.md`   | resumable summary                                           |\n| `drift.md`          | append-only drift log                                       |\n| `phase-registry.md` | supervisor runs only: phase-group map + live status         |\n\nThere is no `commits.md` — the draft-PR commit list + per-slice PR comments are the commit trail.\nKeep `worklog.md` + `context-pack.md` current as part of every slice. Supervisor runs additionally\nkeep `phase-registry.md`, `final-pr-handoff.md`, and an `escalations/` folder; brief each group agent\nwith `templates/agent-briefing.md`.\n\n## `.llm/runs` Path Caveat\n\nSome search/index tools may skip or lag on freshly written `.llm/runs/` run dirs (the same caveat\nthat applied to the old `.llm/tmp/run/` location). Verify run paths with a direct filesystem listing\nwhen needed:\n\n```powershell\ndir /s /b \".llm\\runs\\<id>\" 2>&1\n```\n\n## Resource Aggregation\n\nWhen external docs or examples matter:\n\n1. check `.resources/deps-docs/`,\n2. check `.llm/tmp/docs/`,\n3. fetch official/primary docs with available docs tooling,\n4. save useful extracts to `.llm/tmp/docs/<source>-<topic>.md`,\n5. cite the extract in the run artifact.\n\n## Evaluator Separation\n\nThere are **two** separate-session evaluator passes. Both run on the evaluator lane selected from\n`workflow/lane-policy.md`. The normal local route is a **native opposite-family session**:\nClaude/Fable evaluates Codex-authored work and Codex/Sol evaluates Claude-authored work. OpenRouter\nis an escalation only for a third opinion or when the native evaluator family is quota-blocked;\nOpenHands is reserved for explicitly cloud-driven work. No lane may self-certify.\n\n**PLAN-EVAL** (before implementation):\n\n- Runs only for genuinely complex or decision-heavy work; quick fixes record `PLAN-EVAL: N/A`.\n- Runs in a separate session on the evaluator lane selected from `workflow/lane-policy.md`.\n- Reads `evaluator/plan-protocol.md` + `gates/plan-gate.md`.\n- Reads `research.md`, `plan.md`, and the `## Design` section.\n- Writes `plan-eval.md`.\n- Emits `PASS` or `FAIL_PLAN`.\n- Two `FAIL_PLAN` cycles, then escalate.\n\n**IMPL-EVAL** (final pass, after implementation):\n\n- Runs in a separate session on the evaluator lane selected from `workflow/lane-policy.md`.\n- Generator writes `worklog.md`, `context-pack.md`, and `drift.md`.\n- Evaluator reads `.llm/harness/evaluator/protocol.md`, the plan, worklog, context pack, drift, the\n  draft-PR commit list + per-slice PR comments (the commit trail), selected archetype, overlays, and\n  gate docs.\n- Evaluator writes `evaluate.md` with `PASS`, `FAIL_FIX`, `FAIL_RESCOPE`, or `FAIL_DEBT`.\n- Eval loop limit is two failures before escalation.\n\n## Commit Trail\n\nWhen a run requires commits, the draft PR's commit list + per-slice PR comments **are** the commit\ntrail — there is no `commits.md`. Per slice:\n\n1. commit by implementation slice (message names what the slice proves, not what it contains),\n2. push the branch,\n3. comment on the draft PR with slice scope, commit hash, and gate/test evidence,\n4. update `worklog.md` + `context-pack.md` as part of the same slice (a slice whose commit does not\n   touch the run dir is incomplete),\n5. continue to the next slice.\n\nThe draft PR is opened in the same session as the first commit, so its commit list is live and\nreviewable from mobile without cloning or diffing.\n\n## Rescoping\n\nRescope when the real work is materially larger than the approved plan or when the selected\narchetype is wrong. Confirm with the user before expanding scope.\n\nRecord rescope evidence in `drift.md` with severity `significant` or `architectural`.\n\n## Where Lessons Belong\n\n| Content type                                  | Destination                          |\n| --------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------ |\n| Generic run mechanics                         | `.llm/harness/workflow/`             |\n| Archetype-specific gates or false-done states | `.llm/harness/archetypes/`           |\n| Stable repeated cross-run lessons             | `.llm/harness/lessons/`              |\n| Package/plugin doctrine navigation            | `.claude/skills/netscript-doctrine/` |\n| Deep domain expertise                         | a focused skill                      |\n| Deferred doctrine violations                  | `.llm/harness/debt/arch-debt.md`     |\n\n## Quick Decision Tree\n\n```text\nUser says \"use harness\"\n  -> read workflow/activation.md and workflow/run-loop.md\n  -> resuming? read context-pack.md\n  -> deliverable is a board (epics/issues/milestones from a major feature, refactor, replan,\n     or triage)? read workflow/seed-run.md — drafts only until owner ratification\n  -> deliverable is code for a single scoped change? stay on run-loop.md (a seed run here is\n     ceremony)\n  -> package/plugin? select ARCHETYPE-* and load netscript-doctrine\n  -> frontend/service/docs? apply SCOPE-* overlay\n  -> two or more phase groups? read workflow/supervisor.md + escalation.md, keep phase-registry.md\n  -> read gate matrix + plan-gate.md\n  -> complex/decision-heavy plan or genuine need for adversarial advice? run PLAN-EVAL (separate session); otherwise record PLAN-EVAL: N/A\n  -> supervised? assign lanes per workflow/lane-policy.md (generator != evaluator session)\n  -> slice landed + gates green? Tier-A supervisor reviews before the sign-off commit\n  -> OpenHands/local/cloud handoff? read workflow/agent-handoff.md\n  -> update run artifacts while working\n  -> commit slice? push + comment on the draft PR (the commit trail); keep worklog/context-pack current\n  -> discovered violation not fixed? update arch-debt.md\n  -> evaluator is separate session\n```\n\n## Reference Files\n\n| File                                            | Load when                   |\n| ----------------------------------------------- | --------------------------- |\n| `.llm/harness/workflow/activation.md`           | Every harness run           |\n| `.llm/harness/workflow/run-loop.md`             | Every harness run           |\n| `.llm/harness/workflow/lane-policy.md`          | Lane assignment + model bindings |\n| `.llm/harness/workflow/doc-audit.md`            | Docs-changeset runs — opposite-family Sol audit + Fable prose polish of Claude-generated docs |\n| `.llm/harness/workflow/supervisor.md`           | Multi-group supervisor runs |\n| `.llm/harness/workflow/seed-run.md`             | Planning-only board-seeding runs (discovery → roadmap → owner-ratified filing) |\n| `.llm/harness/gates/plan-gate.md`               | Plan-Gate checklist         |\n| `.llm/harness/evaluator/plan-protocol.md`       | PLAN-EVAL instructions      |\n| `.llm/harness/evaluator/protocol.md`            | IMPL-EVAL instructions      |\n| `.llm/harness/evaluator/verdict-definitions.md` | Verdict meanings            |\n| `.llm/harness/gates/archetype-gate-matrix.md`   | Gate selection              |\n| `.llm/harness/archetypes/README.md`             | Archetype selection         |\n| `.llm/harness/templates/`                       | Run artifact scaffolding    |\n| `.llm/harness/debt/arch-debt.md`                | Debt registry               |\n\n## Checklist\n\n- [ ] `workflow/activation.md` and `workflow/run-loop.md` were read.\n- [ ] Run dir has `supervisor.md` (supervisor identity + lane table) written at run start.\n- [ ] Archetype and overlays are selected and justified.\n- [ ] Lane assignments follow `workflow/lane-policy.md`, or the override is recorded in\n      `supervisor.md`/`drift.md`.\n- [ ] Plan-Gate checklist (`gates/plan-gate.md`) was reviewed.\n- [ ] PLAN-EVAL returned `PASS` before implementation when selected, or a justified `PLAN-EVAL: N/A` was recorded first.\n- [ ] Any PLAN-EVAL used the recorded route, or the owner-authorized fallback was recorded.\n- [ ] Tier-D (WSL Codex) slices recorded daemon-managed proof, thread id, worktree, and steering\n      command.\n- [ ] The slice review gate was performed (Tier-A substantive review) before each sign-off commit;\n      no lane self-certified.\n- [ ] Each implementation slice was committed, pushed, and commented on the draft PR.\n- [ ] IMPL-EVAL is a separate session from the generator.\n- [ ] IMPL-EVAL used the recorded evaluator route (open model), or the blocked launch was recorded.\n"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"0e648836-6433-4f98-b898-fb77cb07112d","uuid":"bdaf8d2d-e3b0-4189-a22a-db0823d10bc1","timestamp":"2026-08-12T07:57:48.187Z","isSynthetic":true}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"chatcmpl-tool-b24c19022cbd16be","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Evaluator Protocol\n2\t\n3\tThis protocol governs **IMPL-EVAL**, the **final** evaluator pass. The Plan-Gate's **PLAN-EVAL** is\n4\ta separate, earlier pass governed by `plan-protocol.md`. Both passes are separate sessions.\n5\t\n6\tThe evaluator is a separate session from the generator. Its job is to verify the approved plan\n7\tagainst the changed state, not to continue implementation.\n8\t\n9\t**Evaluator surface (local vs cloud).** The invariant never changes: the generator session is never\n10\tthe evaluator session, and no lane self-certifies. The transport is how that invariant is realized.\n11\t\n12\t- **Local run (the default for harness work).** PLAN-EVAL and IMPL-EVAL run in fresh native\n13\t  opposite-family sessions: Claude/Fable evaluates Codex-authored work and Codex/Sol evaluates\n14\t  Claude-authored work. The **supervisor** triggers the evaluator; a sub-agent never\n15\t  auto-dispatches one. PLAN-EVAL is conditional for complex/decision-heavy work, while IMPL-EVAL\n16\t  remains mandatory unless the owner explicitly waives it.\n17\t- **OpenRouter escalation.** Use the bound Minimax M3 high PLAN preset or DeepSeek V4 Flash 0731\n18\t  max IMPL preset only for a genuine third opinion or when the native opposite-family route is\n19\t  quota-blocked. Approved open models only; never send Claude/GPT/Gemini through OpenRouter.\n20\t- **Cloud run.** OpenHands dispatch is temporarily paused by owner direction while its trigger path\n21\t  is repaired. Do not dispatch it; use the local evaluator transport. When restored it remains:\n22\t  **open models only (Minimax M3 / Qwen 3.8 Max), cloud-driven runs only; dispatching it with a closed\n23\t  model (Claude/GPT/Gemini) is prohibited — it burns paid OpenRouter credit.**\n24\t- **Ordinary (non-formal) review** — the slice review gate, code/PR review — remains\n25\t  **opposite-family Claude ⇄ Codex**: a Codex session reviews Claude-authored work, a Claude session\n26\t  reviews Codex-authored work, mixed authorship per slice or by both.\n27\t\n28\tSelect the route from `workflow/lane-policy.md` and record it in `supervisor.md`/`drift.md`. See\n29\t`.agents/skills/openhands-handoff/SKILL.md` \"Routing policy\" for the model rules, which are shared\n30\tby both transports.\n31\t\n32\tIf the OpenRouter escalation is blocked by an OpenRouter limit, use only the explicit\n33\towner-authorized fallback: a fresh separate Antigravity (`agy`) session on Google subscription,\n34\tmodel `gemini-3.6-flash-high`, effort `high`. Record the block and requested/observed identity. This\n35\tis not Gemini over OpenRouter and does not weaken evaluator independence.\n36\t\n37\t**Capability (verified, drift D-4 amended).** The evaluator lane is fully capable on this transport:\n38\tboth approved open models return a **real reasoning trace** and have a **verified agentic turn**\n39\t(they make real tool calls through Claude Code), so both phase-bound evaluators **can run gates**\n40\tand their `effort` is genuine — not nominal.\n41\t\n42\t| Model on Claude Code + OpenRouter | Reasoning trace | Agentic turn |\n43\t| --------------------------------- | --------------- | ------------ |\n44\t| `minimax/minimax-m3`              | yes             | supported    |\n45\t| `deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash-0731` | yes             | supported    |\n46\t| `z-ai/glm-5.2` (design lane only) | **none**        | —            |\n47\t\n48\tThe missing-reasoning problem is **GLM-specific, not a client-wide gap**: only GLM 5.2 over\n49\tOpenRouter returns zero thinking blocks. Never cite \"GLM 5.2 · xhigh reasoning\" as gate evidence.\n50\tThat caveat is scoped to the design-verification lane and does **not** apply to the evaluator lane.\n51\t\n52\t## Required Inputs\n53\t\n54\t| Input                                        | Required                    | Purpose                                                |\n55\t| -------------------------------------------- | --------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------ |\n56\t| `workflow/run-loop.md`                       | yes                         | run-loop phases and design checkpoint rules            |\n57\t| `verdict-definitions.md`                     | yes                         | verdict rules                                          |\n58\t| selected archetype profile                   | yes for package/plugin work | doctrine gates, concept of done, and false-done states |\n59\t| selected scope overlays                      | when applicable             | frontend/service/docs gates                            |\n60\t| run `plan.md`                                | yes                         | approved scope                                         |\n61\t| run `plan-eval.md`                           | when PLAN-EVAL selected     | conditional planning verdict                           |\n62\t| run `worklog.md`                             | yes                         | design checkpoint and generator evidence               |\n63\t| run `context-pack.md`                        | yes when present            | resumable state                                        |\n64\t| run `drift.md`                               | yes when present            | plan/doctrine drift                                    |\n65\t| draft-PR commit list + per-slice PR comments | yes when commits exist      | implementation history (the commit trail)              |\n66\t| `debt/arch-debt.md`                          | yes                         | debt delta                                             |\n67\t\n68\t## Operating Rules\n69\t\n70\t1. Evaluate against the approved plan and archetype gates.\n71\t2. Verify either the selected Plan-Gate passed before implementation (`plan-eval.md` = `PASS`) or\n72\t   the run recorded a justified `PLAN-EVAL: N/A` before implementation. Missing both is a process\n73\t   failure.\n74\t3. Verify the Design checkpoint exists in `worklog.md` and commit slices follow it. Missing design\n75\t   evidence is a finding.\n76\t4. Verify each commit slice has its named gate passing.\n77\t5. Check the Concept of Done (run-loop § 5 + archetype profile) for each slice.\n78\t6. The Plan-Gate's **PLAN-EVAL** is\na separate, earlier pass governed by `plan-protocol.md`. Both passes are separate sessions.\n\nThe evaluator is a separate session from the generator. Its job is to verify the approved plan\nagainst the changed state, not to continue implementation.\n\n**Evaluator surface (local vs cloud).** The invariant never changes: the generator session is never\nthe evaluator session, and no lane self-certifies. The transport is how that invariant is realized.\n\n- **Local run (the default for harness work).** PLAN-EVAL and IMPL-EVAL run in fresh native\n  opposite-family sessions: Claude/Fable evaluates Codex-authored work and Codex/Sol evaluates\n  Claude-authored work. The **supervisor** triggers the evaluator; a sub-agent never\n  auto-dispatches one. PLAN-EVAL is conditional for complex/decision-heavy work, while IMPL-EVAL\n  remains mandatory unless the owner explicitly waives it.\n- **OpenRouter escalation.** Use the bound Minimax M3 high PLAN preset or DeepSeek V4 Flash 0731\n  max IMPL preset only for a genuine third opinion or when the native opposite-family route is\n  quota-blocked. Approved open models only; never send Claude/GPT/Gemini through OpenRouter.\n- **Cloud run.** OpenHands dispatch is temporarily paused by owner direction while its trigger path\n  is repaired. Do not dispatch it; use the local evaluator transport. When restored it remains:\n  **open models only (Minimax M3 / Qwen 3.8 Max), cloud-driven runs only; dispatching it with a closed\n  model (Claude/GPT/Gemini) is prohibited — it burns paid OpenRouter credit.**\n- **Ordinary (non-formal) review** — the slice review gate, code/PR review — remains\n  **opposite-family Claude ⇄ Codex**: a Codex session reviews Claude-authored work, a Claude session\n  reviews Codex-authored work, mixed authorship per slice or by both.\n\nSelect the route from `workflow/lane-policy.md` and record it in `supervisor.md`/`drift.md`. See\n`.agents/skills/openhands-handoff/SKILL.md` \"Routing policy\" for the model rules, which are shared\nby both transports.\n\nIf the OpenRouter escalation is blocked by an OpenRouter limit, use only the explicit\nowner-authorized fallback: a fresh separate Antigravity (`agy`) session on Google subscription,\nmodel `gemini-3.6-flash-high`, effort `high`. Record the block and requested/observed identity. This\nis not Gemini over OpenRouter and does not weaken evaluator independence.\n\n**Capability (verified, drift D-4 amended).** The evaluator lane is fully capable on this transport:\nboth approved open models return a **real reasoning trace** and have a **verified agentic turn**\n(they make real tool calls through Claude Code), so both phase-bound evaluators **can run gates**\nand their `effort` is genuine — not nominal.\n\n| Model on Claude Code + OpenRouter | Reasoning trace | Agentic turn |\n| --------------------------------- | --------------- | ------------ |\n| `minimax/minimax-m3`              | yes             | supported    |\n| `deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash-0731` | yes             | supported    |\n| `z-ai/glm-5.2` (design lane only) | **none**        | —            |\n\nThe missing-reasoning problem is **GLM-specific, not a client-wide gap**: only GLM 5.2 over\nOpenRouter returns zero thinking blocks. Never cite \"GLM 5.2 · xhigh reasoning\" as gate evidence.\nThat caveat is scoped to the design-verification lane and does **not** apply to the evaluator lane.\n\n## Required Inputs\n\n| Input                                        | Required                    | Purpose                                                |\n| -------------------------------------------- | --------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------ |\n| `workflow/run-loop.md`                       | yes                         | run-loop phases and design checkpoint rules            |\n| `verdict-definitions.md`                     | yes                         | verdict rules                                          |\n| selected archetype profile                   | yes for package/plugin work | doctrine gates, concept of done, and false-done states |\n| selected scope overlays                      | when applicable             | frontend/service/docs gates                            |\n| run `plan.md`                                | yes                         | approved scope                                         |\n| run `plan-eval.md`                           | when PLAN-EVAL selected     | conditional planning verdict                           |\n| run `worklog.md`                             | yes                         | design checkpoint and generator evidence               |\n| run `context-pack.md`                        | yes when present            | resumable state                                        |\n| run `drift.md`                               | yes when present            | plan/doctrine drift                                    |\n| draft-PR commit list + per-slice PR comments | yes when commits exist      | implementation history (the commit trail)              |\n| `debt/arch-debt.md`                          | yes                         | debt delta                                             |\n\n## Operating Rules\n\n1. Evaluate against the approved plan and archetype gates.\n2. Verify either the selected Plan-Gate passed before implementation (`plan-eval.md` = `PASS`) or\n   the run recorded a justified `PLAN-EVAL: N/A` before implementation. Missing both is a process\n   failure.\n3. Verify the Design checkpoint exists in `worklog.md` and commit slices follow it. Missing design\n   evidence is a finding.\n4. Verify each commit slice has its named gate passing.\n5. Check the Concept of Done (run-loop § 5 + archetype profile) for each slice.\n6. Run or manually verify the applicable gates independently.\n7. Treat missing evidence as a finding.\n8. Name doctrine violations by AP code when possible.\n9. Use `FAIL_DEBT` when the only blocking issue is unrecorded or malformed architecture debt.\n10. Use `FAIL_RESCOPE` when the plan is materially wrong, not merely incomplete.\n11. Do not fix implementation except for minimal read-only validation commands.\n12. Verify the **close-gate** (`netscript-pr` → \"Merge close-gate (#387)\") is honored before any\n    `status:ready-merge` / `Closes #N` merge: for every referenced issue, its acceptance criteria\n    and every `gate:` checkbox are checked with linked evidence, and the PR's Definition-of-Done\n    checklist is complete. An unchecked `gate:` box on a referenced issue (the #260 failure) blocks\n    the pass.\n13. Verify every agent brief/prompt (implementation, evaluation, side-fix) carries a `## SKILL`\n    chapter naming the relevant skills (harness rule; a missing SKILL chapter in a brief is a\n    finding). PR bodies are governed by the `netscript-pr` templates and do NOT require a `## SKILL`\n    chapter — never raise its absence from a PR body as a finding.\n14. For a **cut or release-gating run**, verify the **release-gate class** (`gates/release-gates.md`\n    — `scaffold.runtime`, `e2e-cli-prod`, and the composite release gate) is green with\n    raw-exit-code evidence before any `status:ready-merge` / release. A red or unrun release gate on\n    a release cut blocks the pass. The gate definitions are owned by #309 release engineering (the\n    `netscript-release` skill); the evaluator confirms they ran, it does not redefine them.\n    Non-release runs treat this rule as `n/a`.\n\n## Output\n\nWrite `.llm/runs/<run-id>/evaluate.md` using `templates/evaluate.md`.\n\n## Evidence Standard\n\nEvery `PASS` row must have evidence: command, file, trace, route, consumer path, or debt entry. A\nblank `PASS` is not a pass.\n","numLines":108,"startLine":1,"totalLines":108}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"chatcmpl-tool-b90013b7547f42ec","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Verdict Definitions\n2\t\n3\tEvaluator verdicts are stable values. Use exactly one.\n4\t\n5\t## PASS\n6\t\n7\tUse `PASS` when all are true:\n8\t\n9\t- approved scope is complete,\n10\t- required static gates pass or are not applicable,\n11\t- required fitness gates pass, are not applicable, or have valid debt entries,\n12\t- required runtime and consumer gates have evidence,\n13\t- no unrecorded doctrine violation was introduced or deepened,\n14\t- docs and run artifacts are updated enough for resume.\n15\t\n16\t## FAIL_PLAN\n17\t\n18\t`FAIL_PLAN` is emitted only by PLAN-EVAL at the Plan-Gate. The plan is incomplete or unsound (an\n19\tunchecked `gates/plan-gate.md` box). Returns the run to Plan & Design; no implementation slice may\n20\tbe committed. Two `FAIL_PLAN` cycles, then escalate to the user.\n21\t\n22\t## FAIL_FIX\n23\t\n24\tUse `FAIL_FIX` when the plan remains valid but the implementation or docs need more work. Examples:\n25\t\n26\t- a required gate fails,\n27\t- evidence is missing,\n28\t- a path/link is wrong,\n29\t- a consumer import was not updated,\n30\t- a false-done state from the profile is present.\n31\t\n32\t## FAIL_RESCOPE\n33\t\n34\tUse `FAIL_RESCOPE` when the approved plan is materially wrong or too small. Examples:\n35\t\n36\t- the task requires a package/API redesign not named in the plan,\n37\t- the selected archetype is wrong,\n38\t- a docs run uncovers code reality that needs a separate implementation plan,\n39\t- external/runtime validation cannot be performed without adding new scope.\n40\t\n41\t## FAIL_DEBT\n42\t\n43\tUse `FAIL_DEBT` when code/docs may otherwise satisfy the plan, but architecture debt handling is\n44\tinvalid. Examples:\n45\t\n46\t- a doctrine violation is introduced without an `arch-debt.md` entry,\n47\t- an entry lacks owner, target, reason, linked plan, or status,\n48\t- new work deepens an existing debt entry without updating it,\n49\t- a run closes a debt entry without evidence that the relevant gate now passes.\n50\t\n51\t`FAIL_DEBT` means the required fix is debt bookkeeping or debt rationale, not necessarily\n52\timplementation.\n53\t\n54\t## Choosing Between Verdicts\n55\t\n56\t| Condition                                                    | Verdict        |\n57\t| ------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------- |\n58\t| Plan incomplete or unsound at the Plan-Gate (PLAN-EVAL only) | `FAIL_PLAN`    |\n59\t| Code/docs incomplete, plan valid                             | `FAIL_FIX`     |\n60\t| Plan/archetype/scope invalid                                 | `FAIL_RESCOPE` |\n61\t| Only debt registry is blocking                               | `FAIL_DEBT`    |\n62\t| All applicable gates and debt rules satisfied                | `PASS`         |\n63\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"0e648836-6433-4f98-b898-fb77cb07112d","uuid":"b7a332aa-7ebb-406b-bd31-53d45ea036e9","timestamp":"2026-08-12T07:57:52.034Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/codex/repos/ns006-1405-impleval/.llm/harness/evaluator/verdict-definitions.md","content":"# Verdict Definitions\n\nEvaluator verdicts are stable values. Use exactly one.\n\n## PASS\n\nUse `PASS` when all are true:\n\n- approved scope is complete,\n- required static gates pass or are not applicable,\n- required fitness gates pass, are not applicable, or have valid debt entries,\n- required runtime and consumer gates have evidence,\n- no unrecorded doctrine violation was introduced or deepened,\n- docs and run artifacts are updated enough for resume.\n\n## FAIL_PLAN\n\n`FAIL_PLAN` is emitted only by PLAN-EVAL at the Plan-Gate. The plan is incomplete or unsound (an\nunchecked `gates/plan-gate.md` box). Returns the run to Plan & Design; no implementation slice may\nbe committed. Two `FAIL_PLAN` cycles, then escalate to the user.\n\n## FAIL_FIX\n\nUse `FAIL_FIX` when the plan remains valid but the implementation or docs need more work. Examples:\n\n- a required gate fails,\n- evidence is missing,\n- a path/link is wrong,\n- a consumer import was not updated,\n- a false-done state from the profile is present.\n\n## FAIL_RESCOPE\n\nUse `FAIL_RESCOPE` when the approved plan is materially wrong or too small. Examples:\n\n- the task requires a package/API redesign not named in the plan,\n- the selected archetype is wrong,\n- a docs run uncovers code reality that needs a separate implementation plan,\n- external/runtime validation cannot be performed without adding new scope.\n\n## FAIL_DEBT\n\nUse `FAIL_DEBT` when code/docs may otherwise satisfy the plan, but architecture debt handling is\ninvalid. Examples:\n\n- a doctrine violation is introduced without an `arch-debt.md` entry,\n- an entry lacks owner, target, reason, linked plan, or status,\n- new work deepens an existing debt entry without updating it,\n- a run closes a debt entry without evidence that the relevant gate now passes.\n\n`FAIL_DEBT` means the required fix is debt bookkeeping or debt rationale, not necessarily\nimplementation.\n\n## Choosing Between Verdicts\n\n| Condition                                                    | Verdict        |\n| ------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------- |\n| Plan incomplete or unsound at the Plan-Gate (PLAN-EVAL only) | `FAIL_PLAN`    |\n| Code/docs incomplete, plan valid                             | `FAIL_FIX`     |\n| Plan/archetype/scope invalid                                 | `FAIL_RESCOPE` |\n| Only debt registry is blocking                               | `FAIL_DEBT`    |\n| All applicable gates and debt rules satisfied                | `PASS`         |\n","numLines":63,"startLine":1,"totalLines":63}}}

type {\n  StreamProducerBufferPolicyV1,\n  StreamProducerReadinessOptionsV1,\n  StreamProducerReconnectPolicyV1,\n  StreamProducerStateSnapshotV1,\n  StreamWriteReceiptV1,\n  StreamWriteRejectionReasonV1,\n} from '../domain/producer-contract-v1.ts';\nimport type { StateSchema, StreamStateDefinition } from '../domain/stream-schema.ts';\nimport type { StreamProducerClockPort } from '../ports/stream-producer-clock-port.ts';\nimport type { StreamProducerPort, StreamWriteContextV1 } from '../ports/stream-producer-port.ts';\nimport type { StreamProducerRandomPort } from '../ports/stream-producer-random-port.ts';\nimport type { StreamProducerTransportPort } from '../ports/stream-producer-transport-port.ts';\nimport { DurableStreamProducerTransport } from '../adapters/durable-stream-producer-transport.ts';\nimport { SystemStreamProducerClock } from '../adapters/system-stream-producer-clock.ts';\nimport { SystemStreamProducerRandom } from '../adapters/system-stream-producer-random.ts';\nimport { DurableStreamProducerSupervisor } from './durable-stream-producer-supervisor.ts';\nimport {\n  createStreamsInstrumentation,\n  type StreamsInstrumentation,\n  type StreamsPublishOperation,\n} from '../telemetry/instrumentation.ts';\nimport { buildStreamUrl, getStreamsAuth } from './stream-url-resolver.ts';\n\nexport type { StateSchema, StreamStateDefinition } from '../domain/stream-schema.ts';\nexport type { StreamProducerPort, StreamWriteContextV1 } from '../ports/stream-producer-port.ts';\n\n/** Options accepted by {@link DurableStreamProducer}. */\nexport interface DurableStreamProducerOptions<TDef extends StreamStateDefinition> {\n  /** Stream path relative to the base URL, for example `/workers/executions`. */\n  readonly streamPath: string;\n  /** State schema produced by {@link defineStreamSchema}. */\n  readonly schema: StateSchema<TDef>;\n  /** Stable producer identity for idempotent delivery. */\n  readonly producerId: string;\n  /** Optional finite reconnect policy overrides. */\n  readonly reconnectPolicy?: Partial<StreamProducerReconnectPolicyV1>;\n  /** Optional dual buffer policy overrides. */\n  readonly bufferPolicy?: Partial<StreamProducerBufferPolicyV1>;\n  /** Optional stream instrumentation; defaults to real NetScript telemetry. */\n  readonly instrumentation?: StreamsInstrumentation;\n  /** Optional protocol transport adapter. */\n  readonly transport?: StreamProducerTransportPort;\n  /** Optional backoff clock adapter. */\n  readonly clock?: StreamProducerClockPort;\n  /** Optional reconnect jitter adapter. */\n  readonly random?: StreamProducerRandomPort;\n  /** Optional lifecycle abort signal. */\n  readonly signal?: AbortSignal;\n}\n\n/** Server-side writer for a named durable stream. */\nexport class DurableStreamProducer<TDef extends StreamStateDefinition>\n  implements StreamProducerPort {\n  /** Stream path owned by this producer. */\n  readonly streamPath: string;\n  /** Stable fingerprint used to reject incompatible singleton reuse. */\n  readonly configurationFingerprint: string;\n\n  readonly #schema: StateSchema<TDef>;\n  readonly #producerId: string;\n  readonly #instrumentation: StreamsInstrumentation;\n  readonly #supervisor: DurableStreamProducerSupervisor;\n\n  /** Create a producer and begin connecting under the finite retry policy. */\n  constructor(options: DurableStreamProducerOptions<TDef>) {\n    this.streamPath = options.streamPath;\n    this.configurationFingerprint = producerFingerprint(options);\n    this.#schema = options.schema;\n    this.#producerId = options.producerId;\n    this.#instrumentation = options.instrumentation ?? createStreamsInstrumentation();\n    this.#supervisor = new DurableStreamProducerSupervisor({\n      url: resolveRequiredStreamUrl(this.streamPath),\n      headers: getStreamsAuth(),\n      producerId: options.producerId,\n      transport: options.transport ?? new DurableStreamProducerTransport(),\n      clock: options.clock ?? new SystemStreamProducerClock(),\n      random: options.random ?? new SystemStreamProducerRandom(),\n      reconnectPolicy: options.reconnectPolicy,\n      bufferPolicy: options.bufferPolicy,\n      signal: options.signal,\n      onStopped: () => removeProducer(this.streamPath, this),\n      onState: (snapshot) =>\n        this.#instrumentation.recordState(this.streamPath, this.#producerId, snapshot),\n      onBuffer: (events, bytes) =>\n        this.#instrumentation.recordBuffer(this.streamPath, this.#producerId, events, bytes),\n      onRetry: (phase, attempt, reason) =>\n        this.#instrumentation.recordRetry(\n          this.streamPath,\n          this.#producerId,\n          phase,\n          attempt,\n          reason,\n        ),\n      onRecovery: (phase, attempt) =>\n        this.#instrumentation.recordRecovery(\n          this.streamPath,\n          this.#producerId,\n          phase,\n          attempt,\n        ),\n    });\n  }\n\n  /** Current lifecycle and buffer snapshot. */\n  get state(): StreamProducerStateSnapshotV1 {\n    return this.#supervisor.state;\n  }\n\n  /** Whether writes can currently be attempted without reconnecting first. */\n  get isReady(): boolean {\n    return this.#supervisor.isReady;\n  }\n\n  /** Whether shutdown or terminal failure prevents new writes. */\n  get closed(): boolean {\n    return this.#supervisor.closed;\n  }\n\n  /** Resolve on the current or next ready transition. */\n  waitUntilReady(options: StreamProducerReadinessOptionsV1 = {}): Promise<void> {\n    return this.#supervisor.waitUntilReady(options);\n  }\n\n  /** Upsert an entity into a stream collection. */\n  upsert<K extends keyof TDef & string>(\n    entityType: K,\n    value: Record<string, unknown>,\n    context?: StreamWriteContextV1,\n  ): StreamWriteReceiptV1 {\n    if (this.closed) {\n      return this.#reject(this.#supervisor.writeRejectionReason() ?? 'producer-failed');\n    }\n    const definition = this.#schema[entityType];\n    if (!definition) {\n      return this.#reject('invalid-entity');\n    }\n    const rawKey = value[definition.primaryKey];\n    const key = rawKey === undefined || rawKey === null ? '' : String(rawKey);\n    if (key === '') {\n      return this.#reject('invalid-key');\n    }\n    return this.#serializeAndEnqueue({\n      entityType,\n      operation: 'upsert',\n      key,\n      value,\n      context,\n      type: definition.type ?? entityType,\n    });\n  }\n\n  /** Delete an entity from a stream collection by primary key. */\n  delete<K extends keyof TDef & string>(\n    entityType: K,\n    key: string,\n    context?: StreamWriteContextV1,\n  ): StreamWriteReceiptV1 {\n    if (this.closed) {\n      return this.#reject(this.#supervisor.writeRejectionReason() ?? 'producer-failed');\n    }\n    const definition = this.#schema[entityType];\n    if (!definition) {\n      return this.#reject('invalid-entity');\n    }\n    if (key === '') {\n      return this.#reject('invalid-key');\n    }\n    return this.#serializeAndEnqueue({\n      entityType,\n      operation: 'delete',\n      key,\n      context,\n      type: definition.type ?? entityType,\n    });\n  }\n\n  /** Wait for writes accepted before this call. */\n  flush(): Promise<void> {\n    return this.#supervisor.flush();\n  }\n\n  /** Stop local work without sending durable EOF. */\n  stop(): Promise<void> {\n    return this.#supervisor.stop();\n  }\n\n  /** Drain accepted writes, acknowledge durable EOF, and stop. */\n  close(): Promise<void> {\n    return this.#supervisor.close();\n  }\n\n  #serializeAndEnqueue(\n    input: Readonly<{\n      entityType: string;\n      operation: 'upsert' | 'delete';\n      type: string;\n      key: string;\n      value?: Record<string, unknown>;\n      context?: StreamWriteContextV1;\n    }>,\n  ): StreamWriteReceiptV1 {\n    const publish = this.#startPublish(\n      input.entityType,\n      input.operation,\n      input.key,\n      input.context,\n    );\n    try {\n      const event = JSON.stringify({\n        type: input.type,\n        key: input.key,\n        ...(input.value ? { value: input.value } : {}),\n        headers: publish.headers,\n      });\n      return this.#supervisor.enqueue(event, publish);\n    } catch {\n      return this.#supervisor.reject('serialization-failed', publish);\n    }\n  }\n\n  #startPublish(\n    entityType: string,\n    operation: 'upsert' | 'delete',\n    key: string,\n    context?: StreamWriteContextV1,\n  ): StreamsPublishOperation {\n    const correlationId = context?.correlationId?.trim() || key;\n    const messageId = context?.messageId?.trim() || key;\n    const publish = this.#instrumentation.startPublish({\n      streamPath: this.streamPath,\n      collection: entityType,\n      operation,\n      producerId: this.#producerId,\n      messageId,\n      correlationId,\n    });\n    return {\n      headers: { operation, correlationId, ...publish.headers },\n      event: (name, attributes) => publish.event(name, attributes),\n      finish: (outcome) => publish.finish(outcome),\n    };\n  }\n\n  #reject(reason: StreamWriteRejectionReasonV1): StreamWriteReceiptV1 {\n    this.#instrumentation.recordRejected(this.streamPath, this.#producerId, reason);\n    return this.#supervisor.reject(reason);\n  }\n}\n\nfunction resolveRequiredStreamUrl(streamPath: string): string {\n  try {\n    return buildStreamUrl(streamPath);\n  } catch (error) {\n    const message = error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error);\n    throw new Error(\n      `[DurableStreamProducer] Missing plugin reference \"streams\" for stream \"${streamPath}\". ` +\n        'Install the streams plugin, then run `netscript service generate` to regenerate Aspire wiring. ' +\n        message,\n    );\n  }\n}\n\nfunction producerFingerprint<TDef extends StreamStateDefinition>(\n  options: DurableStreamProducerOptions<TDef>,\n): string {\n  return JSON.stringify({\n    producerId: options.producerId,\n    reconnectPolicy: options.reconnectPolicy ?? {},\n    bufferPolicy: options.bufferPolicy ?? {},\n  });\n}\n\nconst producers = new Map<string, DurableStreamProducer<StreamStateDefinition>>();\n\nfunction removeProducer(\n  streamPath: string,\n  producer: DurableStreamProducer<StreamStateDefinition>,\n): void {\n  if (producers.get(streamPath) === producer) {\n    producers.delete(streamPath);\n  }\n}\n\n/** Create or reuse a compatible durable stream producer for one stream path. */\nexport function createDurableStream<TDef extends StreamStateDefinition>(\n  options: DurableStreamProducerOptions<TDef>,\n): DurableStreamProducer<TDef> {\n  const existing = producers.get(options.streamPath);\n  if (existing && !existing.closed) {\n    const fingerprint = producerFingerprint(options);\n    if (existing.configurationFingerprint !== fingerprint) {\n      throw new Error(`Incompatible durable stream producer options for \"${options.streamPath}\"`);\n    }\n    return existing as DurableStreamProducer<TDef>;\n  }\n  if (existing?.closed) {\n    producers.delete(options.streamPath);\n  }\n\n  const producer = new DurableStreamProducer(options);\n  producers.set(options.streamPath, producer as DurableStreamProducer<StreamStateDefinition>);\n  return producer;\n}\n","numLines":305,"startLine":1,"totalLines":305}}}

Legal lifecycle states for a reconnecting durable stream producer. */\nexport const STREAM_PRODUCER_LIFECYCLE_STATES_V1 = [\n  'connecting',\n  'ready',\n  'backoff',\n  'reconnecting',\n STREAM_PRODUCER_LIFECYCLE_STATES_V1)[number];\n\n/** Finite retry policy used for connection and delivery attempts. */\nexport interface StreamProducerReconnectPolicyV1 {\n  /** Maximum total attempts for one connection or delivery operation. */\n  readonly maxAttempts: number;\n  /** Maximum wall time for one transport request before it becomes retryable. */\n  readonly requestTimeoutMs: number;\n readonly maxDelayMs: number;\n  /** Symmetric delay jitter ratio in the inclusive range 0 through 1. */\n  readonly jitterRatio: number;\n}\n\n/** Default bounded reconnect policy. */\nexport const DEFAULT_STREAM_PRODUCER_RECONNECT_POLICY_V1: StreamProducerReconnectPolicyV1 = {\n  maxAttempts: 8,\n  requestTimeoutMs: 5_000,\n  initialDelayMs: 100,\n  multiplier: 2,\n  maxDelayMs: 5_000,\n  jitterRatio: 0.2,\n};\n\n/** Dual queue bounds for accepted durable stream writes. */\nexport interface StreamProducerBufferPolicyV1 {\n  /** Maximum accepted events that may await acknowledgement. StreamProducerStateSnapshotV1 {\n  /** Current lifecycle state. */\n  readonly state: StreamProducerLifecycleStateV1;\n  /** Current one-based attempt number, or zero when idle. */\n  readonly attempt: number;\n  /** Accepted events that have not received acknowledgement. */\n  readonly bufferedEvents: number;\n  /** UTF-8 bytes retained for those events. */\n  readonly bufferedBytes: number;\n  /** Most recent failure message, when present. */\n  readonly error?: string;\n}\n\n/** Reasons a producer rejects a write before accepting it. */\nexport type StreamWriteRejectionReasonV1 =\n  | 'invalid-entity'\n  | 'invalid-key'\n  | 'serialization-failed'\n  | 'buffer-count-exceeded'\n  | 'buffer-bytes-exceeded'\n  | 'producer-stopping'\n  | 'producer-stopped'\n  | 'producer-failed';\n\n/** Reasons an accepted write is cancelled before its first delivery attempt. */\nexport type StreamWriteCancellationReasonV1 = 'producer-stopped' | 'producer-failed';\n\n/** Reasons an attempted write cannot be reported as delivered or rejected. */\nexport type StreamWriteUnknownReasonV1 =\n  | 'retry-exhausted'\n  /** The transport positively refused the write with a non-retryable failure. */\n  | 'transport-refused'\n  | 'transport-aborted'\n  | 'producer-stopped';\n\n/** Terminal outcome of one durable stream write. */\nexport type StreamWriteOutcomeV1 =\n  | StreamWriteCancellationReasonV1 }>\n  | Readonly<{ status: 'delivery-unknown'; reason: StreamWriteUnknownReasonV1; error?: string }>;\n\n/** Immediate acceptance plus eventual terminal outcome for one {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"chatcmpl-tool-9876598eb1f9f186","type":"tool_result","content":"title:\tfix(streams): durable producer settles two inaccurate rejection reasons — close-drain reports producer-failed, single-attempt refusal reports retry-exhausted\nstate:\tOPEN\nauthor:\trickylabs\nlabels:\tarea:plugins, priority:p2, status:impl-eval, type:fix\ncomments:\t1\nassignees:\t\nprojects:\t\nmilestone:\t0.0.6\nissue-type:\t\nparent:\t\nsub-issues:\t\nsub-issues-completed:\t\nblocked-by:\t\nblocking:\t\nnumber:\t1405\n--\n## Summary\n\n`DurableStreamProducer`'s reconnect supervisor (#1326) settles every write explicitly and meters\nevery drop path — there is no silent loss. But two settled **reason strings** misdescribe the state\nthat produced them.\n\n1. **Close-drain window.** `close()` flips `#accepted = false` before the `stopping` transition\n   (`packages/plugin-streams-core/src/application/durable-stream-producer-supervisor.ts:205-226`), so\n   a write arriving during the drain is rejected as **`producer-failed`** while the producer is in\n   fact healthy and closing (`create-durable-stream.ts:131,251-261`).\n2. **Single-attempt refusal.** A non-retryable append failure settles\n   **`delivery-unknown: retry-exhausted`** even on attempt 1 (`supervisor.ts:412-423`). No retries\n   were exhausted; the server positively refused.\n\n## Why it matters\n\nNeither is a correctness defect and neither can produce a false `delivered` — both were verified safe\nby the separate-session IMPL-EVAL of PR #1402. The cost is diagnostic: a caller or operator reading\n`producer-failed` during an orderly shutdown will look for a failure that did not happen, and\n`retry-exhausted` on a first-attempt refusal hides that the server rejected the write outright.\n\nAccurate reasons are most of the value of settling writes explicitly rather than dropping them.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] A write rejected during the close-drain window settles with a reason naming the closing state,\n      distinct from `producer-failed`\n- [ ] A non-retryable append failure on attempt 1 settles with a reason naming the refusal, distinct\n      from `retry-exhausted`\n- [ ] Retry-exhaustion keeps its own reason for the case where retries genuinely were exhausted\n- [ ] Tests assert each reason for its own state, so a future refactor that collapses them fails\n- [ ] No change to which writes are accepted, rejected or delivered — reasons only\n\n## Provenance\n\nIMPL-EVAL of PR #1402 (#1326), 2026-08-09, findings MINOR-1 and MINOR-2, both explicitly\nnon-blocking. Verdict record:\n`.llm/runs/release-0.0.5--orchestration/slices/w3-a-1326/evaluate.md`.\n\nRefs #1326.","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"0e648836-6433-4f98-b898-fb77cb07112d","uuid":"a357910f-9dcd-427f-9374-d2ea7ac14763","timestamp":"2026-08-12T08:00:21.504Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"title:\tfix(streams): durable producer settles two inaccurate rejection reasons — close-drain reports producer-failed, single-attempt refusal reports retry-exhausted\nstate:\tOPEN\nauthor:\trickylabs\nlabels:\tarea:plugins, priority:p2, status:impl-eval, type:fix\ncomments:\t1\nassignees:\t\nprojects:\t\nmilestone:\t0.0.6\nissue-type:\t\nparent:\t\nsub-issues:\t\nsub-issues-completed:\t\nblocked-by:\t\nblocking:\t\nnumber:\t1405\n--\n## Summary\n\n`DurableStreamProducer`'s reconnect supervisor (#1326) settles every write explicitly and meters\nevery drop path — there is no silent loss. But two settled **reason strings** misdescribe the state\nthat produced them.\n\n1. **Close-drain window.** `close()` flips `#accepted = false` before the `stopping` transition\n   (`packages/plugin-streams-core/src/application/durable-stream-producer-supervisor.ts:205-226`), so\n   a write arriving during the drain is rejected as **`producer-failed`** while the producer is in\n   fact healthy and closing (`create-durable-stream.ts:131,251-261`).\n2. **Single-attempt refusal.** A non-retryable append failure settles\n   **`delivery-unknown: retry-exhausted`** even on attempt 1 (`supervisor.ts:412-423`). No retries\n   were exhausted; the server positively refused.\n\n## Why it matters\n\nNeither is a correctness defect and neither can produce a false `delivered` — both were verified safe\nby the separate-session IMPL-EVAL of PR #1402. The cost is diagnostic: a caller or operator reading\n`producer-failed` during an orderly shutdown will look for a failure that did not happen, and\n`retry-exhausted` on a first-attempt refusal hides that the server rejected the write outright.\n\nAccurate reasons are most of the value of settling writes explicitly rather than dropping them.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] A write rejected during the close-drain window settles with a reason naming the closing state,\n      distinct from `producer-failed`\n- [ ] A non-retryable append failure on attempt 1 settles with a reason naming the refusal, distinct\n      from `retry-exhausted`\n- [ ] Retry-exhaustion keeps its own reason for the case where retries genuinely were exhausted\n- [ ] Tests assert each reason for its own state, so a future refactor that collapses them fails\n- [ ] No change to which writes are accepted, rejected or delivered — reasons only\n\n## Provenance\n\nIMPL-EVAL of PR #1402 (#1326), 2026-08-09, findings MINOR-1 and MINOR-2, both explicitly\nnon-blocking. Verdict record:\n`.llm/runs/release-0.0.5--orchestration/slices/w3-a-1326/evaluate.md`.\n\nRefs #1326.","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"chatcmpl-tool-8ca0168f5dc49a50","type":"tool_result","content":"# Worklog — #1405 durable producer rejection taxonomy\n\n## Design\n\n### Public surface\n\n- Add exactly `transport-refused` to `StreamWriteUnknownReasonV1`.\n- Reuse the existing `producer-stopping` rejection reason during graceful close drain.\n- Keep the existing root re-exports; add no entrypoint or export.\n\n### Domain vocabulary and ports\n\n- Closing intent is private supervisor state, visible from `close()` entry until shutdown settles.\n- A non-retryable transport failure is a positive refusal; retryable failure at the attempt bound is\n  exhaustion.\n- Existing transport, clock, random, queue, and lifecycle ports remain unchanged.\n\n### Constants\n\n- No new constant collection is required; the published reason unions remain the finite vocabulary.\n\n### Commit slice\n\n- S1 changes only reason selection and adds deterministic close/refusal/exhaustion tests. Proving\n  gates are the focused tests plus the complete gate set named in the slice brief.\n\n### Deferred scope\n\n- No acceptance, delivery, cancellation, retry-count, telemetry-classification, scaffold, #1398,\n  merge, or release changes.\n\n### Contributor path\n\n- Start with `producer-contract-v1.ts` for reason vocabulary, then follow supervisor settlement and\n  rejection selection into the contract behavior tests.\n\n## Phase status\n\n- `PLAN-EVAL: N/A` — the owner brief and orchestrator research fully specify the two selectors,\n  locked vocabulary, negative tests, boundaries, and gates.\n- Implementation and generator gates complete on the assigned `light_implementation` lane.\n- Separate orchestrator-owned slice review and IMPL-EVAL remain pending.\n\n## Evidence\n\n| Gate | Exit | Result |\n| --- | ---: | --- |\n| Focused contract behavior | 0 | 8 passed, 0 failed; includes all four new negative tests. |\n| Existing telemetry classification guard | 0 | 2 passed, 0 failed. |\n| Scoped check wrapper | 0 | 43 files, 1 batch, 0 failures/occurrences. |\n| Scoped lint wrapper | 0 | 43 files, 1 batch, 0 occurrences. |\n| Scoped format wrapper | 0 | 43 files, 1 batch, 0 findings. |\n| Post-format reason grep | 0 | `transport-refused` and `producer-stopping` remain in contract, selector, and tests. |\n| `deno task quality:gate` | 0 | `quality:scan` and `arch:check` completed; existing repository warnings only. Bounded FIFO admission and exactly-once local receipt settlement. */\nexport class DurableStreamProducerQueue {\n  readonly #policy: StreamProducerBufferPolicyV1;\n  readonly #onBuffer?: (events: number, bytes: number) => void;\n  readonly #entries: ProducerQueueEntry[] = [];\n  #bufferedBytes = 0;\n  #receiptId = 0;\n\n  /** Create a queue under fixed dual bounds. */\n  constructor(\n    policy: StreamProducerBufferPolicyV1,\n    onBuffer?: (events: number, bytes: number) => void,\n  ) {\n    this.#policy = policy;\n    this.#onBuffer = onBuffer;\n  }\n\n  /** Current FIFO entries. this.#policy.maxBytes\n    ) {\n      return rejectedReceipt(id, 'buffer-bytes-exceeded', lifecycle);\n    }\n    const deferred = createProducerDeferred<StreamWriteOutcomeV1>();\n
